# DISCOURSE I.

### The difference between a PHILOSOPHER and a CHRISTIAN.

*What a philosopher is; they pass for good Christians; nevertheless, it is the Spirit of CHRIST alone that makes the Christian.*

It seems to me <note>original: "Methinks"</note> that a philosopher is a man who examines everything deeply by the light of his moral reasoning. He insists on knowing the cause and the reason for everything that is proposed to him—whether it is right and reasonable to act one way or another. He believes nothing but what he sees and that which is clearly demonstrated to him. Since it appears to him a very plain thing—and one which perfectly aligns with his moral reasoning—that we must avoid and abstain from vice, and on the contrary practice virtue, hating the one and loving the other: if such a philosopher is upright and sincere,

<summary>This page defines a philosopher as one who relies on individual moral reasoning and logical demonstration to distinguish between virtue and vice.</summary>
<keywords>philosophy, Christianity, moral reason, virtue, vice, logic, Spirit of Christ</keywords>

--- Page 176 ---
<meta>This page continues the definition of a "philosopher," focusing on how such an individual uses moral reasoning to control their passions and live a virtuous life, often outperforming self-proclaimed Christians in their conduct.</meta>

<page-num>4</page-num>

<header>Difference between a PHILOSOPHER</header>

He will study by all means to practice what his moral reason is convinced of; he will not allow <note>original: "suffer"</note> his passions to take control <note>original: "bear sway"</note>, but will bridle and keep them under restraint by the strength of his understanding and moral reasoning. These persuade him that it is a shame to follow one's passions and allow them to gain the mastery over him.

Here, in a few words, is the philosopher. This is how a rational and sincere man governs himself in his conduct. The more exact he is in following the light that shines in the darkness of human nature—and which produces his moral reason—the more regular and precise you shall find him in all his actions. This is what made so many pagan <note>original: "heathen"</note> philosophers, who have been held in admiration on account of their doctrine and conduct, practice virtue and combat vice to a far greater perfection than many others who call themselves Christians.

Among these latter <note>original: "last"</note> people, there are still some persons who endeavor to live virtuously in the same manner that those others have done. Through the conviction their moral reason affords them, they study by all means to lead a wise and rational life; and as the Christian religion affords us a most complete system... <meta>catchword: system</meta>

<summary>This page describes the philosopher as one who uses reason to master their passions and live virtuously. It notes that many ancient pagans achieved higher moral standards through reason than many modern Christians.</summary>

<keywords>philosopher, moral reason, passions, virtue, vice, heathen philosophers, Christian religion, self-mastery</keywords>

--- Page 177 ---
<meta>This page continues the comparison between a "philosopher" and a "Christian," explaining how the moral reasoning of a wise person aligns with Christian teachings to the point that such individuals are often mistaken for devout Christians.</meta>

<page-num>5</page-num>

<header>->and a CHRISTIAN.<-</header>

<meta>bleed-through from reverse page visible; catchword: tianity,</meta>

system of morality, which the doctrine of our Lord **JESUS CHRIST** teaches us in the highest perfection. The moral reasoning of wise men agrees exceedingly well with this doctrine up to a certain point; therefore, any wise man who applies himself—even if only partially <note>original: "faintly"</note>—to live a virtuous life and does not follow the loose, unbridled conduct of libertines <note>term: libertine; a person who rejects moral boundaries and lives purely for pleasure</note>, such a man, I say, is immediately considered by us to be a good Christian. The more effort <note>original: "pains"</note> he puts in, the more diligent <note>original: "assiduous"</note> he is, and the more he strives to follow the paths of virtue, the more he is looked upon as a good Christian.

We know of no Christianity that requires <note>original: "engages us to"</note> a different kind of behavior; this conduct is excellent and approved by all, and passes for being the true Christian religion. Indeed, this level of practice is rarely found among the multitudes that profess what we call the Christian faith. There are very few who carry the practice of it to such a high degree. Those who do so, and employ all the strength of their understanding to gain a rational conviction that they ought to fight <note>original: "combat"</note> their vices and practice virtue, are greatly to be esteemed.

Meanwhile, although in truth the majority <note>original: "generality"</note> of Christians know little other Christianity...

For I beg you, tell me: is mere historical knowledge of **JESUS CHRIST**, and of what we read that He has externally done for us; the practice of a few outward ceremonies; and all this, combined with a life that does not even come close to the virtue which some heathens have practiced with far greater perfection—will these things ever make a Christian? 

"Certainly they will," you will say; "what more would you have?" 

Alas! It is most true that people in general are sunk in such a dreadful corruption, and allow themselves to be led by their corrupt passions, without shame, into all sorts of vice, that it is not surprising if a man is accounted a good Christian who applies himself, though weakly <note>original: "faintly"</note>, to the practice of virtue. But where is the spirit of Christianity? In short, what is that spirit? And how can it be proved that such wise men <note>term: sages; profound philosophers or scholars</note> do not have it?

<page-num>7</page-num>

<header>and a CHRISTIAN.</header>

...so extraordinary a liberality that one would be apt to call it charity <note>term: charity; here meaning "agape" or divine love</note> in a most eminent degree, since it goes so far as to give all one’s goods to the poor; [it speaks] of a zeal for the confession of one’s belief, or one’s faith, which rises so high as to give one’s body to be burnt. Who is it that pushes his devotion and his zeal this far? And yet the Apostle says that without charity, "all is nothing: I am nothing." What then is this charity, O holy Apostle? He lists its effects very specifically; but as to what it is in itself, the beloved disciple *John* informs us, saying, *God is love* (1 John 4:8). It is God Himself, then, who is charity, and we do not have charity if we do not have God in us. 

It is the *love of God, His Word, His Spirit*—all are one—for *if anyone does not have the Spirit of Christ, he does not belong to Him* (Romans 8:9, 14). This, then, is what makes a Christian: specifically, His Spirit. Those who are moved or led by the Spirit of God *are the children of God*. It is the Spirit in a person that makes them what they are. Here, then, is the distinguishing characteristic of a Christian: he is one in whom the Spirit of God dwells, whom it moves, governs, and leads, as the Apostle *Paul* says: *if the Spirit of Christ dwells in you, etc.*

This Spirit must be the principle <note>original: "principle"; here meaning the fundamental source or origin</note> of life from which all our actions and operations flow, so that they may be pleasing to God and accepted by Him. It is this Spirit, then, that makes a person a Christian. It renders all the actions and virtues of the man who is ruled and governed by it into Christian virtues and actions, because they flow from a divine principle.

They did this without reflecting, reasoning, or giving a single thought to their worldly <note>original: "temporal"</note> affairs.

# DISCOURSE II.

### Concerning the fall of *Adam*, and that JESUS CHRIST is the Redeemer and sole Teacher within the new man. <note>original: "new man"; a biblical term referring to the regenerated spiritual nature of a believer (Ephesians 4:24).</note>

*Concerning the fall of* Adam; *how* JESUS CHRIST *took the glorious body of* Adam *into his own most glorious body, and afterwards took on his miseries; the purpose for which* JESUS CHRIST *came clothed in our flesh; that through him* GOD *the Word* <note>original: "GOD the Word"; refers to the Logos, the eternal Son of God described in the Gospel of John.</note> *knocks at the heart and is the only Teacher for those who are willing to learn from him.*

MOSES writes that *God created man in his own image and breathed the breath of life into his nostrils, and man became a living soul* (Genesis 2:7). But the moment *Adam* disobeyed his GOD, he fell into death because GOD withdrew the divine life he had breathed into his nostrils at his creation. Since GOD is spirit, he shared <note>original: "communicated"</note> his own spirit with *Adam* through that breath. This was the spiritual man, sharing the same nature as GOD and being his image, because through him

As for Adam's earthly <note>original: "terrestrial"</note> body, it received its life from earthly and vital spirits; the "astral man" <note>original: "astral man"; in the mystical philosophy of the era, this refers to the rational mind and intellect, which was believed to be influenced by the "astral" or starry heavens.</note>—the power and faculty of reasoning—derives its life from the astral spirit. # Redemption by Jesus Christ, and that in him

...of the tree of knowledge of good and evil, *you shall die* (Genesis 2:17). This sentence was carried out the very moment our first father fell. This sentence applies to all his descendants; for as soon as a person falls into themselves—into their own self-will <note>original: "propriety"; in this context, it refers to the state of being one's own master or acting out of self-interest rather than divine will.</note>—the spiritual person, born of the promised Word <note>original: "inspoken seed"; the divine spark or promise of redemption placed within humanity after the fall.</note>, loses its authority over them. Unless one is born again through repentance, this spiritual nature is separated and withdrawn from the soul when the physical body dies <note>original: "breaking off of the earthly body."</note>.

It was then that this divine Word of divine love—in whom the full essence of the divine nature <note>original: "godhead"</note> lives, and who is the exact representation of <term>God</term>—out of pure grace, undertook the redemption of humanity and became the mediator between <term>God</term> and man: the very Son of <term>God</term>! In <term>Jesus Christ</term>, at the appointed time, He was revealed in the body prepared for Him and said, "*I know, Father, that you always hear me*" (John 11:42). This does not mean the Son of <term>God</term> is separate or distinct from the Father; He is His love and His delight. Whatever the Son desires—whatever His love asks for—the Father always grants and performs, because it is His own will. Likewise, everything desired by those in whom <term>Jesus Christ</term> lives and has become their life, they also receive; for it is the love of <term>God</term> itself that asks for it.

--- Page 191 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the divine Word as the mediator and redeemer. Having established that the Son of God acts out of love to fulfill the Father's will, the text now describes the Incarnation—how the Word took on human form and suffering to reconcile humanity to God after the fall of Adam.</meta>

->### God the Word is the sole Teacher.<-

In this Word, the Son of God came at the appointed time and took our nature upon Him, both soul and body—the very garment with which sin has covered humanity. He did this so that, as the Son of Man, He might suffer and die amid the most bitter pains and the greatest ignominy <note>original: "ignominy"; deep public shame or disgrace.</note>. All this was to rescue us from that unparalleled misery into which humanity had plunged itself through rebellion. He was the "man of sorrows," despised and rejected by men; He bore our griefs, was wounded for our transgressions, and was bruised for our iniquities <note>original: "iniquities"; sins or gross injustices.</note>. The punishment for sin was laid upon Him, and through Him, our peace was obtained. The iniquity of us all—stemming from the transgression of *Adam*—was laid upon Him, and by His stripes <note>original: "stripes"; lashes or wounds from a whip.</note>, we are healed. To those who receive Him, the adorable Savior and Redeemer, He gives the power to become the children of God.

This eternal Word came clothed in our misery. He took the same organs and faculties we have and covered Himself with the same mortal body that we carry as a punishment, in order to make Himself accessible <note>original: "communicable"; here meaning able to be perceived by, shared with, or reached by human beings.</note> to us. Because He took upon Himself the heaviest burdens out of love for us, and voluntarily bore the misery to which we are naturally bound in this mortal life, He requires that we should not treat our imprisonment here as a place of delights and pleasures. It is true that we may have been instructed from our youth in the **Holy Scriptures** and in the truths of the **Christian religion** by those who were responsible for our education, by the good books that may have fallen into our hands, and by teachers who have taught us outwardly. His words are spirit and life! He enables the will to share in the light which he imparts to the understanding, and he engraves what he speaks there into the heart. This word penetrates into the innermost depths of the soul, and the soul is compelled to confess the truth: that the Word in **Jesus Christ** is the sole teacher and the only master.

-> ### Redemption by Jesus Christ, and that in Him ### <-

Among those who are taught by **God** in this way, there is no disagreement. They have learned from Him how to put His instructions into practice. They have given Him their hearts and would rather be obedient to what He commands than learn all the doctrines of the gospel through human skill and systematic methods. 

They focus their efforts on love rather than intellectual speculation. They would rather open their hearts to be filled with the fire of divine love than stuff their heads with a great deal of academic learning, or with many opinions and systems. Among these people, I say, we find a consistency of feeling, regardless of the era in which they lived or the religious society or sect with which they were connected. 

The **Holy Spirit** purified them from "dead works" <note>original: "dead works"; refers to external religious rituals or actions performed without faith or spiritual life</note>, led them to true repentance and a change of life, and caused them to abandon their sins, vices, and wicked habits. The Spirit regenerated them so that **Jesus Christ** might live and rule alone within them. In this entire <term>economy of grace</term> <note>original: "oeconomy of grace"; refers to the divine plan or system by which God governs the world and offers salvation to humanity</note> which brings about spiritual rebirth, a complete harmony has been found among everyone, even though they have never seen or known each other.

### DISCOURSE III.

### The difference between the life of NATURE and the life of GRACE.

->*That by the fall, the Holy Spirit is lost; and that we recover it in* JESUS CHRIST *by regeneration.*<-

In the previous discourse, we have seen man whole and complete, just as God created him in his own image and likeness. We have also seen how, by turning away from God—which was the essence of his fall—he lost the most noble part of himself: the spiritual and divine man. This is what the Apostle Paul calls the Spirit, to which man died. As the same Apostle writes to the Christians, speaking of the state they were in before their conversion: "*and when you were dead in your sins and trespasses*" (*Ephesians 2*), Christ has raised you up. He gave them life <note>original: "quickened"; in this 17th-century context, "quickened" means to be made alive or to have life restored</note> by restoring the divine life to them, which is that Spirit—the new creature <note>original: "new creature"; often translated in modern Bibles as "new creation" (2 Corinthians 5:17)</note>.

I remember talking one day with a very learned and well-known theologian <note>original: "divine"</note>, who was famous for his wisdom, about how the Spirit of <term>God</term> works within us. To clear up and explain this matter, we must examine what exactly we call "reason" in human beings. This is especially necessary because reason is currently the great idol and the god that the "wise" and "sensible" people of this world worship. They recognize nothing higher in humanity, since they use this faculty to distinguish humans from wild animals <note>original: "brute beasts"</note>, claiming that "man is a rational creature."

This is a very inadequate <note>original: "lame"</note> definition—though it is an accurate and, unfortunately, all too true description of humanity in its fallen state. In this condition, man is spiritually dead, a stranger to the divine life, and lacking the new birth of that creature "born of water and of the Spirit" (John 3:5), which is the only way a soul can enter the kingdom of <term>God</term>. In this fallen state, that definition might apply; it would be even better to say that man is a creature endowed with moral reason through a light that shines in every person born into the world.

Because of this, he is more enlightened and more refined than animals <note>original: "beasts"</note>, which show through their actions and instincts that they are not completely lacking in natural reason—provided we do not blind ourselves with false and subtle reasonings, moving entirely away from simplicity through a hollow <note>original: "vain"</note> philosophy that darkens and confuses the mind, and is actually ridiculous. 

For example, where does it come from that a dog, or any other animal, has the ability to be taught, trained, and disciplined by humans? If we judge naturally, without over-complicating things and according to what is obvious and plain before our eyes, is this not a demonstration of reasoning in animals? But empty philosophy hurries us along blindly from one absurdity <note>original: "extravagance"</note> to another, which we then label as "wisdom" and "learning."

Let us look at where reason is located. Reason, or reasoning, is an operation of the understanding—or its result and product. It reasons based on the subject presented by the "light" of the understanding, which then determines and moves the will toward what it finds reasonable. This is all fine, provided that the understanding (the place where images and ideas present themselves) is itself enlightened

However, because we have lost this divine nature, our understanding—which is the "eye of the soul"—is clouded with darkness. It has no light left except what is shared with it through the influence of external nature, out of which our natural self is formed or created. Since this nature was infected with the malice <note>original: "malignity"</note> of Satan by the fall, the influences and lights our understanding receives from it must necessarily be very corrupt.

It will fill you with inner joy through the Holy Spirit and make you begin the ever-blessed life of eternity while still in this life. This is the grace which <term>GOD</term> offers to you, and which I wish for you, as one who has, by divine generosity, received a foretaste of it.

# DISCOURSE IV.

### Concerning the Miracles of the Old and New Testaments, and How the Will of <term>GOD</term> May Be Known.

> *Miracles of great display are for people in a sensual state <note>original: "sensual state"; here meaning a state where a person relies primarily on their physical senses rather than spiritual intuition</note>. People should be on their guard regarding outward miracles, as those occurring at present are worked in the spirit and in the secret of the human heart. The power and will of <term>GOD</term> display themselves in the center of the soul.*

The more gross, sensual, and focused on outward things people have been, the more extraordinary and grand the miracles have been that <term>GOD</term> has used to convert them and reveal Himself to them. Conversely, the more spiritual people become, the less <term>GOD</term> makes use of those extraordinary <note>original: "signal"</note> miracles to establish His church.

->This<-

<summary>This page introduces Discourse IV, arguing that while God used grand, outward miracles to reach people focused on the physical world in the past, He now primarily works through internal, spiritual miracles within the human soul.</summary>
<keywords>miracles, Old Testament, New Testament, spirituality, internal transformation, divine will, sensual state</keywords>

<header>Old and New Testament.</header>

This is evident under the <term>Jewish economy</term> <note>original: "Jewish oeconomy"; the system of laws and religious order established for the Israelites</note>, when God established and founded that church. Because those people were extremely coarse, focused on their senses, and concerned entirely with outward appearances, he performed shining miracles in their favor in *Egypt* and in the wilderness. Afterward, they conquered the land of *Canaan* through similar miracles and in a most wonderful manner. In this way, God authorized and established that first external church. The religious service he prescribed for them was directed toward the same goal, and the external ceremonies were increased to suit their condition; they had need of them because they were incapable of understanding spiritual things.

When our Lord Jesus Christ came into the world, he established his religion and likewise authorized it by the miracles he performed. Although these were impressive, as were those which his apostles performed after him, they fell far short in terms of outward display compared to those that *Moses* had performed in *Egypt* and in the desert. The passage through the Red Sea and through the *Jordan* River were events of unparalleled grandeur and majesty. Our blessed Savior performed miracles that were equally noble and marvelous, but not in such a majestic manner, so that his disciples would not focus purely on

the exterior. Our Lord performed only as many miracles as were necessary to release those who believed in him from Jewish regulations <note>original: "Jewish precepts"</note>, and to authorize his status as the Messiah and the Savior of the world. The shame, contempt, and death which he and his apostles suffered also diminished the brilliance <note>original: "lustre"</note> of his miracles in the eyes of men who looked only at outward appearances. Instead, the power of his Spirit working in their hearts was the effective <note>original: "efficacious"</note> means that he employed to convert people; even those miracles which he and his apostles performed outwardly soon came to an end.

At present, now that his kingdom is to be established in the Spirit, we see even fewer of those outward miracles appear. All of his wonderful work is done in secret, and in the silence of the inner heart. The things that make the most show and the greatest noise outwardly are not those of the greatest value in the sight of God, nor are they produced by his pure Spirit. The more the reign of the Holy Spirit is extended and established in hearts, the less will those men who live only by their physical senses find anything to convince or satisfy them through those senses, and

I do not speak here of the will of <term>God</term> in general—that which invites us to repentance, to stop doing evil and learn to do well, to abstain from sin and vice, and to practice virtue. For it is beyond all doubt and hesitation that we must abstain from the one and practice the other: namely, virtue and goodness.

But I speak here of particular cases that occur daily and unexpectedly to a soul devoted to <term>God</term> and desiring more and more to depend upon him in all things. To such a person, a thousand little things are presented, as well as some of importance, which in themselves are neither good nor bad and become such only by the principle <note>original: "principle," here referring to the internal motive or the driving force behind an action</note> that sets them in motion. # How the Will of God may be known.

that it is often difficult to discern what is the will of **God**, and to distinguish it from that which only has the appearance of it.

Well-intentioned souls <note>original: "Well disposed souls"</note> would gladly acquire certainty in these matters in various ways. Some of these people find the certainty upon which they make their decisions from the advice given to them, or from the decision of a person in whom they confide and whom they believe to be in a better position to know the will of **God** than themselves. Others base their decisions on a specific word spoken to them internally, which makes itself understood through an impression on their internal senses. They take this impression—though falsely—to be the <term>inward man</term> <note>the spiritual or hidden self, as opposed to the physical person</note>; and they mistake the voice that pronounces these words for the voice of **God**. Others <term>cast lots</term> <note>a method of making a decision by random chance—such as drawing straws—based on the belief that God would oversee the result</note> and determine their path that way. 

In all these ways of seeking to know the will of **God**, there may be something good, and therefore they should not be rejected. I believe their certainty or truth depends on the heart and disposition of the person who wants to know **God's** will. Nevertheless, there may be a strong element of delusion in these methods, as daily experience proves.

# How the Will of God may be known.

I believe, therefore, that the certain way to know the will of **God** is, first, to have a heart free from every other desire and intention. One must offer oneself without reservation to that holy will, with no other goal than to follow it, and without regard for profit or personal interest of any kind. In a word, we must establish as a foundation a will that is entirely free from all bias—one that keeps **God** alone and his will purely and solely in view. 

Assuming this foundation is in place, it will be easy for a soul, in all kinds of situations, to know the will of **God** or what he requires of her. This is true if she surrenders herself to his providence in external matters or allows <term>second causes</term> <note>natural events or human actions that God uses to fulfill his plan</note> to act, accepting from the hand of **God** what happens through other people <note>original: "by means of the creatures"</note> or from events that often occur unexpectedly. 

In addition to this, she must pay attention to the deep and secret inclination or instinct of her heart and what it moves her toward. If she is a person already advanced in her spiritual life, this instinct of the heart will not fail her. This is because it is her <term>inmost ground</term> <note>the deepest, most essential part of the soul where God is thought to dwell</note>—where **God** resides—that inclines her. She will find by experience that **God** will direct her through his providence, or manage all things from the outside in such a way that her internal attraction or 
instinct

<keywords>divine will, providence, second causes, spiritual instinct, inmost ground, surrender, discernment</keywords>

# How the Will of **God** may be known.

The instinct of her inmost ground <note>original: "inmost ground"; the deepest, most essential part of the soul where God is thought to dwell</note> may have its effect. She will wait patiently until everything is unraveled, will rush nothing <note>original: "precipitate nothing"</note>, and will remain balanced and free from every personal desire and every inclination. 

**God** often—in order to purify her and to make her flexible and pliant—will permit the state of affairs to change many times. This happens so that she is tossed on all sides, tempted, and afflicted in every possible way; a thousand contrary events may befall her, which seem to work in the exact opposite direction of what she knew and what her inward bias inclined her toward. 

But if she remains in tranquility, though without consolation <note>original: "comfortless"; in spiritual writing, this refers to a state of "dryness" where one feels no emotional or spiritual joy</note>, and resigned to everything—always with the intention of renouncing all self-will in order to follow the will of **God** without preference or reservation—**God** will conduct her safely. He will himself accomplish that holy will which she desires, which is the only reason she has surrendered herself and is resigned to all events.

But let us take good heed to ourselves: let us present ourselves before **God** with sincerity and without hypocrisy! We who say constantly, "Oh, that I knew the will of **God**, I would obey it cheerfully!" Let us examine ourselves, I say—we who pretend to...

His will may be done on earth as it is in heaven.

How many mistakes <note>original: "oversights"</note> and false steps are made in this matter, even with the best intentions in the world? But if we inquire into the cause, we will find that we have had our own interest in view, and that it has mixed into the affair. I mean our own *spiritual* interest—that zeal, that sensitivity, and that taste <note>original: "relish"; here meaning an emotional or personal satisfaction</note> for inner feelings <note>original: "internal senses"</note> that usually seduces the mind. It is a self-centered desire to do something for the glory of **God**, but within that desire, the wish to be someone important and to make a name for oneself <note>original: "making some figure"</note> in spiritual matters—both in one’s own eyes and in the eyes of others—is secretly the principal motive that sets us to work. 

To this may be added a thousand suggestions of Satan under the guise of piety <note>original: "pious pretexts"</note>, as well as the delusions of virtuous people who, without knowing it, hurry us on to do something that has a glittering appearance, but which is motivated not by the pull of the Spirit of **God**, but by our own human spirit.

Therefore, a soul that is free from all self-centered desire—one that clings to **God** alone and desires nothing but Him and the accomplishment of His will by His Holy Spirit within us—is what is necessary in order to do or to know the will of **God**.

-> <page-num>62</page-num> <-

That people are so blind, so full of self-love, and so focused on their own interest, that they refuse to recognize any harm in all of this. They take the help and patience of **God**—the protection they often experience when mischief is diverted from falling on their heads, and their being preserved from it—along with being inclined by the urging of their conscience toward what is less evil in the sight of **God** than what they otherwise would have done; they take all these things as so many signs that they are in high favor with **God**, are His favorites <note>original: "darlings"; here meaning those specially beloved or favored</note>, and that He leads them according to His will. 

But in this they are much mistaken. What **God** does for them is only out of great pity and condescension <note>original: "condescension"; meaning God’s graciousness in stooping down to human weakness</note>, in the hope that they will improve and be entirely converted to Him, and not allow themselves to be extremely blinded by their self-love in flattering themselves about the goodness of their spiritual state.

# DISCOURSE V.

### Concerning the union of the church of JESUS CHRIST, and of true religion.

> Restoring the image of **GOD** within us is the foundation of the religion which **JESUS CHRIST** brings about <note>original: "operates"</note>. This concerns the spiritual union of the children of **GOD**, for which everyone ought to prepare themselves by rooting out <note>original: "extirpation"</note> all selfishness and the very source of vice. When the kingdom <note>original: "reign"</note> of **GOD** is within us, we reach this union; and in the person who submits himself entirely to **GOD**, He establishes the union which is not made by any external thing, but in **GOD** alone.

The apostle **Paul** says that no one can lay any other foundation than **JESUS CHRIST** (1 Corinthians 3:11). It is **CHRIST** within us who is the "new creature," as He Himself told **Nicodemus**: "*Unless a man is born again, he cannot see the kingdom of God*" (John 3:3).

<summary>This page introduces Discourse V, which explains that the foundation of true religion is the restoration of God's image in the soul and the spiritual union of believers. It emphasizes that this union is an internal process achieved by rooting out selfishness and submitting entirely to God's kingdom.</summary>
<keywords>Jesus Christ, St. Paul, Nicodemus, spiritual union, new creature, kingdom of God, restoration of God's image, self-denial, true religion</keywords>

<page-num>67</page-num>
<header>and of the true RELIGION.</header>
<sig>K 5</sig>

The foundation of our Christian religion, then, is nothing other than regeneration, which consists in dying to the old self <note>original: "old man"</note> so that we may live for the new. It is to become a new person, *created after God in righteousness and true holiness* (Ephesians 4:24). 

This new self is nothing other than **JESUS CHRIST** being formed within us, changing us so that we are renewed in his image. By this, we give **GOD** the justice due to him by restoring what we have usurped from him. We have committed these usurpations both by acting as our own masters <note>original: "proprietors"</note> and by claiming for ourselves authority <note>original: "privilege"</note> over all other creatures, desiring to use them according to our own will and choice. 

By restoring all this to him and entering once again into our dependence on him, we do him the justice he is owed; in doing so, we give him the opportunity to create the new self within us. This renewal <note>original: "renovation"</note>, then, is the foundation and goal of all religion. It is what **GOD** aims for in everything he proposes to us in the gospel and throughout the Holy Scriptures. It is certain that, provided he achieves this goal of reviving his image within us—which is **JESUS CHRIST**, the new man—it is all he requires.

<summary>This page defines the foundation of Christian religion as regeneration, or "dying to the old self," to allow Jesus Christ to be formed within the soul. It argues that true religion involves surrendering self-will and restoring God's rightful authority over our lives and the world.</summary>
<keywords>regeneration, old man, new man, Jesus Christ, renovation, gospel, Holy Scripture, justice of God, spiritual renewal, self-will</keywords>

<page-num>73</page-num>
# and of the True Religion.

It is in this way that the union of the members of Jesus Christ is formed: all who allow themselves to be possessed by his Spirit—who have welcomed that Spirit into their hearts through a sincere desire and a firm will to renounce all things and their own self-will—these people are united with one another by the Spirit of Jesus Christ dwelling within them. 

As that Holy Spirit takes possession of the entire soul, driving out the "old man" <term>The "old man" (Latin: *vetus homo*) is a biblical term referring to a person’s former, corrupt nature or sinful self before spiritual rebirth.</term> until he is entirely banished, or rather, entirely dead—and as that work progresses toward perfection—these souls are increasingly united with Jesus Christ and with each other. They share in the same Spirit that moves and governs them. 

For this reason, the apostle says, *For all who are led by the Spirit of God are children of God* (Romans 8:14). How, then, could there be any division among them? This is as impossible as it would be for a body given life by a single spirit to be divided against itself, or for the individual parts of that body to be at odds with one another.

<summary>The author explains that true Christian unity arises from the Holy Spirit possessing the soul and displacing the "old self." This internal transformation creates a natural harmony among believers, making division as impossible as a single body fighting itself.</summary>

<keywords>Jesus Christ, Holy Spirit, spiritual union, old man, Romans 8:14, Christian unity, internal transformation</keywords>

Therefore, if we wish to see union flourish among the children of **GOD**, and if we desire for all division and misunderstanding to cease, let us all firmly resolve to renounce ourselves and all external things, our selfish dispositions, our most subtle passions, and the roots of those vices that are so deeply implanted in our natures. Let us surrender ourselves to **JESUS CHRIST** once more, without reservation, so that he may direct us according to his will and prepare us by the work of his Holy Spirit to become his permanent home, so that he may reign in us as sovereign Lord. 

For this purpose, let us follow all the promptings <term>original: "motions"</term> of our conscience and the inward drawing <term>original: "attraction"</term> of **GOD'S** Spirit, which constantly invites every soul—according to the stage of conversion it is in—to practice self-denial. Let us cast off all care and concern for every other thing, as if it does not belong to us, and we shall soon perceive the reign of our Savior **JESUS CHRIST** flourishing and settling within us, and in all those who follow this path without hypocrisy—

What has until now been treated by most people as fanaticism, enthusiasm <note>original: "enthusiasm"; in this period, the word often referred to a misguided or dangerous belief in personal divine inspiration</note>, and idle fancies (except for those few souls who have known the internal ways of the **LORD**) will soon be recognized as the universal Spirit of the church. This is the Spirit of **JESUS CHRIST**, which should give life to and guide His children. They will no longer be dead children, with—

<summary>This page argues that true spiritual union with God and other believers is an internal experience that transcends physical distance and sensory perception. # TREATISES
## By JAKOB BÖHME <note>original: "Jacob Behmen"</note>

->On the Mixed World and its Wickedness.<-

->A Dialogue Between an Enlightened Soul and an Unenlightened Soul, showing the way from darkness to true illumination.<-

->A Compendium of Repentance, or the key which opens divine mysteries <term>mysteries: hidden spiritual truths or revelations known only through divine grace</term>.<-

->On True RESIGNATION <term>resignation: in Böhme's theology, the act of surrendering one's own will and "self-hood" to the will of God</term>.<-

# THE AUTHOR’S
# PREFACE,
### TO THE
### READER who loves GOD.

If you will use this book correctly and are in good earnest <term>original: "earnest"—a deep, sincere, and active spiritual commitment</term>, you shall certainly find the benefit of it. However, I desire that you may be warned: if you are not in good earnest, do not meddle with the dear names of GOD—in which and by which the Most High is named, stirred, and powerfully desired—lest they kindle the anger of GOD in your soul; for we must not abuse the holy names of GOD. This little book is only for those who would gladly <note>original: "fain"</note> repent and have a desire to begin. Both sorts <note>Referring to those who wish to repent and those who are just beginning the process.</note> will find what kind of words are within and where they originate <note>original: "whence they are born"</note>. I commend you to the eternal goodness and mercy of GOD. Amen.

<summary>In this preface, the author warns the reader to approach the book with sincere spiritual intent, specifically cautioning against the casual or insincere use of the holy names of God.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, piety, repentance, holy names, spiritual earnestness, divine mercy</keywords>

# MIXED WORLD,

->AND ITS<-

### WICKEDNESS;

#### What it is now, and how it rules.

->*This is a mirror* <note>original: "glass"</note> *in which everyone may see themselves and discover what kind of spirit's child they are.*<-

1. Christ said, "*O Jerusalem, Jerusalem, how often would I have gathered your children together, as a hen gathers her chickens under her wings, but you were not willing?*" Also, "*O Jerusalem, you who kill the prophets and stone those who are sent to you*" (Matthew 23:37). 2. In this way, the Word, or Wisdom of God, then lamented and complained about the wicked children of humanity. But what would she <note>In biblical literature, such as the Book of Proverbs, Wisdom is personified as a woman.</note> do now in these times? She is now in great sorrow and lamentation, and has turned her face away from these wicked children. She will no longer tolerate them in the garments they now wear. She cries out, and there is no one who hears her. She is in great sorrow, weeping and wailing over the wickedness of these evil and unruly children. Yet everyone runs after that prostitute, Greed <note>original: "whore, covetousness"</note>, which is full of all vice, wickedness, and abominations. 5. Are not the children of both sexes, who run up and down playing together, full of venom and devilish wickedness, and do not all vices and abominations appear in them? # Of the mixed WORLD,

Nevertheless, the needy must exhaust themselves <note>original: "consume his sweat"</note> for his sake and for his pleasure. No pity, ease, or rest can be found from him, and even his dog has a better life than the needy soul living under his roof. 

Yet he considers all of this to be fair, just, and right, even though it is not based on nature. Instead, it is rooted in the dark abyss or bottomless pit, where one form or property of darkness afflicts, vexes, torments, and tortures another, and where life is an enemy to itself.

11. He thinks that if he did not act this way, he could not feed himself <note>original: "fill his belly"</note> through righteous living. Then his own reason goes along with it and tells him he is forced to act this way—without love or righteousness—just to survive. He learns to indulge in wild behavior <note>original: "riotousness"</note>, luxury, and extravagant feasting <note>original: "junketings"</note>...

<meta>This page continues the author's critique of the social hierarchy, specifically how the corruption of the powerful trickles down to those beneath them, and warns of the divine judgment awaiting those who exploit their positions of authority.</meta>

12. Consider this: how will you be able to survive <note>original: "subsist"</note> when GOD shall judge the secrets of men in His passion <note>original: "zeal"</note>? For then the cause of everything that every wicked person has done—or has had anything to do with—will appear and curse them. Everything will set its own motive <note>original: "cause"</note> before the person, and they shall feel it in their conscience. How will those of you who are superiors be able to endure when your subordinates <note>original: "inferiors"</note> cry out, "Woe, woe to you!" for giving them the opportunity to be immoral <note>original: "lewd"</note> and unrighteous? They will say you have robbed them of the fruits of their hard work <note>original: "bereft them of their sweat"</note>, and so forced them to give in to unrighteousness and to embrace worthless things <note>original: "vanity"</note>. 

How will you be able to give an account of your office in which you have been set and placed? You were meant to prevent injury and unrighteousness and to keep the wicked in check by reprimand <note>original: "reproof"</note>, correction, and punishment. But you have not cared to prevent their wicked ways; you have only cared for your own wicked greed <note>original: "covetousness"</note> and how you might—

<summary>The author warns that superiors who model vice and exploit their workers will be held accountable during God's judgment, as their subordinates will testify against them for forcing them into a life of sin and poverty.</summary>

13. And when the severe judgment of <term>God</term> appears, and all works are revealed in the fiery essences <note>original: "fiery effences"; in theosophical writing, this refers to the underlying spiritual reality or "burning" truth of a person’s inner nature</note>—when all things shall be tested in the fire—what do you think? Will not all such works remain in the fire? Then the poor soul will cry out against its ungodly, cursed labor, words, and actions committed while in the body. One person will curse another for giving them the opportunity to do such evil. The torment of falsehood will boil up within the soul and gnaw at it; for it will be a great anguish to that person that they have fooled away such a great glory for the sake of frivolous, trifling vanity and false delights.

<summary>The author argues that corrupt superiors exploit their subordinates' labor while ignoring their spiritual health, leading to a final judgment where both parties will suffer and blame one another for their mutual downfall.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's description of the soul’s self-inflicted torment in the afterlife, where the sins and "abominations" of its earthly life become its eternal source of suffering and internal conflict.</meta>

<page-num>14</page-num>
<header>Of the Mixed WORLD,</header>

14. All malice, scorn, covetousness, pride, and deceit will boil up in the soul, and one torment will continually ignite <note>original: "kindle"</note> and gnaw at the other that caused it. For example, where pride or covetousness has caused unrighteousness, that unrighteousness will gnaw, tear, and curse that which caused it. Then the soul will consider, "If it were not for this abomination, I might attain grace." When it thoroughly weighs and considers itself, it will find that one abomination always begot and brought forth another; it shall perceive that it is nothing but a stinking, loathsome abomination in the presence of <term>God</term>. 

Then, in its anguish and torments, it will cast itself inward into its own center and curse <term>God</term> for having created it to be a soul. The deeper it desires to throw itself, the deeper its fall is, and yet it must still remain in the place of its abominations. It cannot escape from there; the hellish matrix <note>original: "matrix"; a Latin term meaning "womb" or "source," used here to describe the generative environment of hell</note>, or womb, holds it captive. And so, it must feed itself with aching anguish, cursing, abominations, and bitterness—even with those things which the heart has produced <note>original: "wrought"</note> here in this life. In these things, it finally despairs, and that is its everlasting food.

<summary>This page describes the psychological and spiritual state of a damned soul, which becomes trapped in a cycle of self-hatred and internal torment where past sins continually "gnaw" at the conscience.</summary>

15. All earthly food and pleasure perishes at the end of days, or time, and all return again into the ether <note>original: "ether, or receptacle"; in this context, the formless cosmic space or the primal medium from which all material things are thought to arise and return</note>: but the will in man, and the desire within that will, remains forever.

16. Therefore, whether you are old or young, parents or children, superiors or subordinates <note>original: "inferiors"</note>, take note and observe: you have filled Mother Nature <note>original: "the mother of nature"; the generative power of the physical world</note> with all kinds of abominations and with all manner of wickedness. The fierce wrath of <term>God</term> is at hand; the Last Judgment is at the door. <term>God</term> will purge the earth with fire and give everyone their due reward. The harvest is coming, and everything will be gathered and carried into its proper barn. He who will take no advice, let him go on; he shall soon feel what the opening of the seventh seal <note>A reference to the Book of Revelation in the Bible, signifying the final phase of divine judgment and the conclusion of the current world order</note> will bring with it, in its center, or at the end.

17. When human reason looks upon things, it says, "I do not see that things are now any different than they formerly were. Besides, the world has always had good and bad in it, as historians tell us, and men must do as they do, or else they would be complete fools and laughingstocks to the whole world, and be forced to starve."

<summary>The author warns that while physical pleasures are temporary, the human will is eternal, and a divine judgment is approaching to purge the world's wickedness. He also acknowledges the skeptical perspective of "reason," which argues that the world has always been a mix of good and evil and that worldly pragmatism is necessary for survival.</summary>

18. If a man does not give his children the freedom to learn the fashions and ways of the world, they would be nothing but mocked and despised. Also, if a man is not somewhat conventional in his behavior <note>original: "carriage"</note>, and does not take upon himself some status and fine display <note>original: "bravery"; in the 17th century, this referred to ostentatious or expensive clothing and an outward show of wealth</note>, he thinks he will not be respected; and unless he uses some trickery <note>original: "device"</note> to get ahead, it often happens that he cannot maintain that lifestyle. He says, "I am certain to gain nothing through truth, love, and righteousness, but will die a beggar; I must, therefore, do as others do, and then I shall be able to live among them. Why should I be the only fool in all the world? If I do commit sin, <term>God</term> is gracious and merciful; <term>Christ</term> has slain sin and death upon the cross and taken away the power of the devil. I shall repent one day, well enough, and be saved."

19. This is the rule that the world follows; this is the path of both the superior and the inferior, of the shepherd and of the sheep. # DIALOGUE
### Between an enlightened Soul and an unenlightened Soul.

->*Showing the Way from Darkness to true Illumination.*<-

***

THERE was a poor Soul that wandered out of Paradise and came into the kingdom of this world, and there the Devil met with it and asked, "Where are you going, you Soul that is half blind?"

->THE SOUL SAID,<-

I want to go see and examine <note>original: "speculate"</note> the creatures of the world which the Creator has made.

->THE DEVIL SAID,<-

How will you look upon them when you cannot know their essence and nature <note>original: "property"</note>? You will look upon the outside only,

->as<-

<summary>This page introduces an allegorical dialogue where a soul, wandering from Paradise into the world, is challenged by the Devil regarding its ability to truly understand the essence of God's creation.</summary>
<keywords>allegory, soul, Paradise, the Devil, spiritual perception, illumination, essence, creation</keywords>

--- Page 295 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Soul and the Devil, where the Devil employs arguments reminiscent of the serpent's temptation in Eden to lure the Soul into seeking forbidden knowledge of the material world.</meta>

<page-num>23</page-num>
<header>to true ILLUMINATION.</header>
<sig>M 5</sig>
<meta>catchword: THE</meta>

as upon a graven image, and cannot know them thoroughly.

### THE SOUL.

How can I come to know their essence and nature <note>original: "property"</note>?

### THE DEVIL.

Your eyes would be opened to see them thoroughly if you only ate from that source from which the creatures have come to be both good and evil. Then you would be as God himself is and would know what the creature truly is.

### THE SOUL.

I am currently a noble and holy creature, but if I were to do so, I would die, as the Creator has said.

### THE DEVIL.

No, you would not die at all; instead, your eyes would be opened, and you would be like God himself, possessing both good and evil. You would also be mighty, powerful, and very great, as I am. All the complexity <note>original: "subtilty"</note> that exists within the creatures would be revealed to you.

### THE SOUL.

If I had the knowledge of nature and of the creatures, I would then rule the whole world according to my own will.

<summary>In this passage, the Devil tempts the Soul by promising that eating from the source of good and evil will grant it god-like knowledge and power over the natural world. The Soul, initially hesitant due to God's warning of death, begins to entertain the idea of ruling the world through its own will.</summary>

### THE DEVIL.

The whole basis of that knowledge lies within you: simply turn your will and desires away from God and goodness, toward nature and created things <note>original: "the creature"</note>. Then a longing <note>original: "lust"</note> to taste will arise within you, and so you may eat of *the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil*, and thereby gain the knowledge of all things.

### THE SOUL.

Well then, I will eat of the tree of knowledge of good and evil, so that I may rule all things by my own power, and be my own master <note>original: "lord of myself"</note> on earth, doing whatever I wish, just as God himself does.

### THE DEVIL.

I am the Prince of this world; and if you would rule on earth, you must turn your desire toward my image, wanting to be like me, so that you may gain the cunning, wit, reason, and shrewdness <note>original: "subtilty"</note> that my image possesses. And in this way, the Devil presented to the Soul the power that exists in the fire—the very root of nature <note>original: "creature"</note>—in the form of a serpent.

### THE SOUL.

Look, this is the power which can do all things; how can I obtain it?

<page-num>25</page-num>
<header>to true ILLUMINATION.</header>

### THE DEVIL.

You yourself are such a fiery principle; if you break your will away from GOD and focus your desire on this art <note>The "art" or method of self-sovereignty and natural power</note>, then your hidden inner nature <note>original: "ground"</note> will be revealed within you, and then you may also act in the same manner. But you must eat of that fruit in which each of the four elements <note>The classic elements: earth, air, fire, and water, which were believed to compose all material things.</note> tries to rule over the others and are in conflict—heat fighting against cold, and cold against heat—so that all the properties of nature operate through the senses <note>original: "feelingly"</note>. Then you will be like the fiery wheel <note>In the philosophy of Jacob Boehme, the "fiery wheel" represents the restless, revolving cycle of natural forces when they are not balanced by divine light.</note>, and so bring all things into your own power and possess them as your own.

->The Soul did so, and here is what happened as a result:<-

And when the Soul thus broke its will away from GOD and directed its desire into the fiery wheel, a craving <note>original: "lust"</note> immediately arose to eat of the tree of the knowledge of good and evil, and the Soul ate of it.

->As soon as it had done so:<-

All the qualities of nature woke up within the Soul and followed their own cravings and desires.

First, the craving for Pride arose—the desire to be great, mighty, and powerful, to bring all things into subjection at its command, and

<summary>The Devil explains that by turning away from God, the Soul can activate its own inherent "fiery" power and rule over the conflicting elements of nature. The Soul gives in to this temptation, eats from the Tree of Knowledge, and immediately experiences the awakening of Pride and selfish desire.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, fiery wheel, four elements, Pride, fall of man, self-will, Tree of Knowledge, spiritual transformation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the description of the Soul’s fall into a self-centered state after choosing to follow its own will rather than God's. It details the emergence of the three primary "lusts" or fallen cravings: Pride, Covetousness (Greed), and Envy.</meta>

<page-num>26</page-num>
<header>The Way from DARKNESS,</header>

to be its own lord, without control; to despise humility and equality; to esteem itself alone as prudent, witty, and cunning; and to account as folly everything that does not conform to its own way.

Secondly, there arose the craving of greed <note>original: "lust of Covetousness"</note>—a desire for acquisition that seeks to draw all things to itself and into its own possession. For when the craving for pride had turned the Soul’s will away from <term>GOD</term>, the life of the Soul would no longer trust <term>GOD</term> for its needs. Instead, it sought to provide for itself, and therefore directed its desire toward created things <note>original: "creatures"</note>—specifically toward the earth, metals, trees, and other beings. Thus, the ignited fiery life became hungry and greedy once it had broken away from the unity, love, and gentleness <note>original: "meekness"</note> of <term>GOD</term>. It drew to itself the four elements <note>The classic elements of Earth, Air, Fire, and Water, which were believed to make up the material world.</note> and their essence, bringing itself into a bestial state <note>original: "beastiality"; here meaning a state driven by animal instincts rather than divine reason</note>. Consequently, the Soul's life became obscure, dark, empty, and full of wrath; the heavenly virtues and colors faded away, like an extinguished candle.

Thirdly, a stinging, thorny craving awoke within the fiery life: namely, envy. This is a hellish poison and a characteristic shared by all devils; it is a torment that makes the Soul's life an enemy to <term>GOD</term> and to all created beings.

<summary>This page details how the Soul, by turning away from God, falls into the three "lusts" of pride, greed, and envy. This spiritual decline causes the Soul to lose its divine light and become dominated by material elements and animalistic instincts.</summary>

--- Page 299 ---
<meta>This page continues the description of the Soul's fall from grace, detailing how the internal qualities of envy and anger completed its transformation into a state of spiritual darkness and "bestiality."</meta>

<page-num>27</page-num>
<header>to true ILLUMINATION.</header>

This envy rages furiously within the desire of greed <note>original: "covetousness"</note>, like the sting of poison in the body. Envy cannot endure the success or well-being of anyone else; it is malicious and hates whatever greed cannot claim for itself. In this way, the noble love of the Soul was smothered.

Fourthly, a torment like fire awakened in this fiery life—namely, Anger—which sought to murder and destroy everything that would not submit to pride. Thus, the very foundation of hell, which is called the "wrath of God" <note>original: "anger of Gᴏᴅ"</note>, was fully revealed in this Soul. Because of this, the Soul lost the paradise of God and the kingdom of heaven. It became like the fiery serpent that the Devil presented to the Soul in his own image and likeness. 

Consequently, the Soul began to rule on earth in a bestial <note>original: "beastial"; meaning animalistic or driven by base instincts rather than divine reason</note> manner and did everything according to the Devil's will. It lived in mere pride, greed, envy, and anger, no longer possessing any true love toward God. In its place, there arose a wicked, bestial love characterized by filthy lust <note>original: "lechery"</note>, self-indulgence <note>original: "wantonness"</note>, and vanity. There was no more purity in the heart, for the Soul had abandoned paradise and taken the earth into
its

The soul took possession of these earthly desires; its mind was completely focused on cunning, worldly knowledge, subtlety <note>original: "subtilty"</note>, and acquiring a vast amount of material things. No righteousness or virtue remained within it at all. Whatever evil or wrong it committed, the Soul now cleverly and subtly covered it up under the disguise of its own power, authority, and the Law! It called these actions "Right" and "Justice" and considered them to be good.

### The Devil came to the Soul.

At this point, the Devil drew near to the Soul and led it from one vice to another. He had captured the Soul within his own nature <note>original: "essence"</note> and tempted it with joy and pleasure. He said to the Soul, "Look now, you are powerful, mighty, and noble. Strive to be even greater, richer, and more powerful. Use your knowledge, intelligence <note>original: "wit"</note>, and subtlety so that everyone will fear you and stand in awe of you, and so that you may be respected and gain a great reputation in the world."

### The Soul did so.

The Soul followed the Devil's advice, yet it did not realize it was the Devil speaking. Instead, the Soul believed these thoughts came from its own knowledge, intelligence, and understanding, and that it was doing very well and acting correctly all along.

### JESUS CHRIST met with the Soul.

While the Soul continued in this way of life, our dear and loving Lord Jesus Christ—carrying both the love and the wrath of God—came into this world to destroy the works of the Devil and to execute judgment upon all ungodly acts. At a specific time, He met with the Soul and spoke to it with a powerful voice—namely, through the merit of His Passion <note>original: "Passion"; referring to the suffering and crucifixion of Christ</note> and Death. He destroyed the Devil’s work within the Soul and revealed the way toward God’s grace. He shone His mercy upon the Soul, calling it to return and repent, promising that He would deliver it from the monstrous, deformed shape, mask <note>original: "vizard"</note>, or image it had acquired, and would bring it into Paradise once again.

### How CHRIST worked <note>original: "wrought"</note> in the Soul.

When the spark of God’s love, or the Divine Light, was revealed within the Soul, the Soul immediately saw that its own will and actions were in Hell, under the wrath of God. It found that it had become a misshapen, ugly monster in the presence of God and the Kingdom of Heaven. This sight so terrified the Soul that the deepest anguish awoke within it, for God’s judgment was now being revealed in its very heart.

### What Christ said.

Upon this, the Lord Christ spoke to it with the voice of His grace and said, "Repent and forsake vanity, and you shall attain My grace."

### What the Soul did.

Then the Soul—in its ugly, misshapen form <note>original: "image"</note> and wearing the defiled coat of vanity—went before God and pleaded <note>original: "intreated"</note> for grace and the pardon of its sins. It was strongly convinced <note>original: "persuaded"</note> within itself that the satisfaction and atonement of our Lord Jesus Christ belonged to it. 

However, the evil properties of the serpent—formed in the astral spirit <note>In the theology of writers like Jacob Böhme, the "astral spirit" refers to the psychological and intellectual part of a human that connects the eternal soul to the physical body; it is the seat of worldly reason.</note> or the reason of the outward man—would not allow <note>original: "suffer"</note> the will of the soul to come before God. Instead, these properties brought their own lusts and inclinations into the soul. For those evil properties would not die to their own desires, nor would they leave or forsake the world; they were of the world and originated from it. Therefore, they feared the shame of the world if they should forsake their worldly honor and glory.

But the poor Soul turned its face <note>original: "countenance"</note> toward God and desired grace from Him—specifically, that God would bestow His love upon it.

### The Devil came to it again.

### The Soul sighed.

The poor Soul was even more frightened by this—that it could not bring its desires to God—and it began to pray more earnestly. ### The Devil's opposition.

But the Devil opposed it and held it back, so that it could not bring itself into any greater fervor <note>original: "fervency"</note> or repentance.

The Devil awakened the earthly lusts in its heart so that its inclinations might still keep their evil nature, or false natural rights, and set them at odds against the will and desire of the Soul. For these inclinations would not die to their own will and light, but instead wanted to maintain their worldly <note>original: "temporal"</note> pleasures. Thus, they kept the poor Soul captive in their evil desires so that it could not move, even though it sighed and longed ever so much for the grace of <term>GOD</term>.

Whenever the Soul prayed or tried to press forward toward <term>GOD</term>, the lusts of the flesh swallowed up the rays and the short, fervent prayers <note>original: "ejaculations"</note> that went out from the Soul. They dragged them away from <term>GOD</term> into earthly thoughts, so that the Soul might not partake of divine strength. Then the Soul thought it was forsaken by <term>GOD</term> and did not know that <term>GOD</term> was so near, drawing it in this way. Also, the Devil drew even closer to the Soul and entered into the fiery wheel <note>In the theology of the mystic Jacob Böhme, the "fiery wheel" represents the core of the soul's life and the restless drive of its desires.</note> of its life, and mingled its desires with the earthly lusts of the flesh. He mocked the poor Soul and said to it through its earthly thoughts, "Why do you..."

### The condition of the Soul.

In these and similar thoughts, the Soul was ensnared by the Devil and brought into the lusts of the flesh and earthly desires. It was, as it were, bound with fetters and chains and did not know what to do. It looked back a little into the world and its pleasures, but still felt within itself a hunger for divine grace. It would rather enter into and always remain in repentance so that it might find favor with **GOD**. 

For the hand of **GOD** had touched and bruised <note>In this context, "bruised" refers to a spiritual wounding or "contrition," where the heart is broken by the realization of its own sinfulness</note> it; therefore, it could rest nowhere. Instead, it always sighed within itself for sorrow over the sins it had committed and would gladly <note>original: "fain"</note> be rid of them. Yet it could not achieve true repentance, much less a full knowledge of sin; and yet it had such a genuine hunger and longing desire for repentance and sorrow for sin.

->The distressed Soul answered.<-

The Creator has hidden his face <note>original: "countenance"</note> from me, so that I cannot come to his rest. Though I long for him ever so much, I am kept back and cannot partake of his power, virtue <note>original: "vertue"; in this context, it refers to divine efficacy or moral power</note>, and strength, even though I sigh, long, and wait for him.

->The enlightened Soul said.<-

You bear the monstrous shape of the Devil—specifically, the serpent—and are clothed with it. In this shape, the Devil has an entrance into you, for it is his own property. In that state, he keeps your will from penetrating into GOD. For if your will could penetrate into GOD, it would be anointed with the highest power and strength of GOD through the resurrection of our Lord JESUS CHRIST. That anointing <note>original: "unction"</note> would then break into pieces that monster within you, and your original image of paradise would be revealed <note>original: "manifested"</note> in you again. Then the Devil would lose his power over you, and you would become an angel again. But because the Devil envies you this happiness, he holds you captive

### The distressed Soul terrified.

At this speech, the poor, distressed Soul was so terrified and stunned <note>original: "amazed"</note> that it could not speak another word. It perceived that it was in the shape and image of the serpent, which separated the Soul from <term>GOD</term>. It realized that the Devil was very near to it in that image, mingling evil thoughts into the Soul's will and wielding great power over it. The Soul felt it was so near to damnation—stuck fast in the abyss, the bottomless pit of hell, and under the anger of <term>GOD</term>—that it would have despaired of <term>GOD’S</term> grace, if not for one thing. It was upheld by the power, virtue <note>original: "vertue"; meaning divine efficacy or strength</note>, and strength of the first stirring of <term>GOD’S</term> grace. This grace had "bruised" the Soul <note>The "bruising" refers to the painful process of conviction of sin, breaking the pride of the "serpent" within</note>, yet it also supported the Soul so that it did not completely lose hope. 

And so, the Soul wrestled within itself between hope and doubt. Whatever hope built up, doubt threw down again. It was in such continual restlessness <note>original: "disquietness"</note> that, at last, the world and all its glory became loathsome to it. It no longer wished to enjoy the pleasures of this world, and yet, despite all this, it could not find rest.

### The distressed Soul terrified.

At this speech, the poor distressed soul was so terrified and amazed that it could not speak another word. It perceived that it was in the shape and image of the serpent, which separated the soul from GOD. It saw that the Devil was so near to it in that image, and mingled evil thoughts into the will of the soul, having so much power over it because of this. The soul realized it was so near damnation and stuck fast in the abyss—the bottomless pit of hell—within the anger of GOD. 

It would have despaired of the grace of GOD, if not for the power, virtue <note>original: "vertue"; meaning divine efficacy or moral power</note>, and strength of the first stirring of GOD's grace. This grace, which had "bruised" the soul <note>An allusion to the "bruising of the serpent" in Genesis 3:15; here, the soul's ego is being crushed so it can be healed</note>, upheld it so that it did not despair. And so the soul wrestled within itself between hope and doubt; whatever hope built up, doubt threw down again. It was in such continual turmoil <note>original: "disquietness"</note> that at last the world and all its glory became loathsome to it. It no longer wished to enjoy the pleasures of this world, yet despite all this, it still could not find rest.

### The enlightened Soul returns.

At one time, the enlightened soul returned to this soul. Finding it in such great trouble, anguish, and grief of mind, it said: "What are you doing? Do you wish to destroy yourself in your anguish and sorrow? Why do you torment yourself by your own power and will—you who are but a worm <note>A common biblical metaphor for human insignificance and humility before the divine</note>—when you see that your torment only increases more and more because of it? 

If you were to sink yourself to the bottom of the sea, fly to the farthest reaches of the morning, or raise yourself above the stars, you still would not be released. For the more you grieve, torment, and trouble yourself, the more painful your nature will become, and yet you still cannot find rest. 

For your power is completely lost. Just as a dry, withered stick burned to a coal cannot grow green or sprout again by its own power, nor draw sap to flourish alongside other trees and plants, you also cannot reach the presence of GOD by your own power and strength. You cannot transform yourself back into your original angelic image <note>The state of perfection the soul held before falling into sin</note> which you once possessed. In relation to GOD, you are withered and dry—like a parched plant that has lost its sap and strength—and so you have become a dry, tormenting hunger. Your internal qualities <note>original: "properties"; referring to the fundamental forces or "drives" of the soul's nature in Böhme’s theology</note> are like heat..."

->**The Distressed Soul said:**<-

->**The Enlightened Soul answered:**<-

Right now, you possess temporal <note>original: "temporal"; meaning temporary, worldly, or relating to time rather than eternity</note> honor and worldly goods as your own, to do whatever you wish with them. You also enjoy the pleasures of the flesh, and you do not care what you do with these things or how you obtain them. Even though you see the poor and needy—who require your help and are your brothers—you do not help them. Instead, you lay heavy burdens upon them by demanding more from them than they can afford to give. You trouble them by forcing them to toil and labor for you, while you take your pleasure in it.

Furthermore, you are proud and insult them; you are rough, irritable <note>original: "crabbed"</note>, and stern toward them. You exalt yourself above them and think very little of them compared to yourself. Then, that poor, troubled brother of yours comes and sighs toward God because he cannot enjoy the fruit of his own labor and pain, but is forced by you to live in misery.

### The Practice of the Poor Soul.

When the poor soul began to practice this path <note>original: "course"</note> with such earnestness, it imagined <note>original: "conceived"</note> it would obtain the victory immediately <note>original: "presently"</note>. However, the gates of heaven were shut against it when it relied on its own strength and power. It felt as if it had been left and forsaken by God, receiving not even a single ray, look, or glimpse of grace. 

Then the soul thought to itself and said, "Surely you have not sincerely submitted yourself to God. Desire nothing at all from God, but submit yourself wholly to His judgment, sentence, and condemnation, so that He may kill your evil inclinations. Sink down into Him beyond the limits of nature and the created world <note>original: "creature"; here meaning everything that is not the Creator</note>, and submit yourself to Him, so that He may do with you what He will; for you are not worthy to speak to Him."

It forgot all its own actions, feeling as though it would surrender itself to death and cease to exist as a creature any longer. And so, it yielded itself to death, desiring nothing else but to die and perish in the death of its **Redeemer**, **JESUS CHRIST**, who had suffered such torments and death for the soul's sake. In the midst of this "perishing," the soul began to sigh and pray very deeply within itself for the mercy of **GOD**, sinking down into **GOD’S** absolute <note>original: "meerest"; in this context, it means "purest," "absolute," or "total," rather than "insignificant"</note> mercy.

Upon this, the lovely countenance <note>original: "amiable countenance"; the kind or friendly face/appearance</note> of **GOD’S** love appeared to the soul and shone through it like a great light. Because of this, the soul became exceedingly joyful and began to pray properly, thanking the **Most High** for His grace. It rejoiced greatly at being delivered from the death and anguish of hell; there, it tasted the sweetness of **GOD** and His promised truth. 

In that very moment, all the evil spirits that had previously afflicted the soul—keeping it back from the grace, love, and inward presence of **GOD**—were forced to leave. # The Way from DARKNESS,
->to true ILLUMINATION.<-

So it was that reason appeared as foolishness to itself and felt abandoned. The soul did not know what had happened to it, nor whether it was true that it had tasted the divine light of grace or not. Yet it could not stop struggling, for the burning fire of love had been sown within it. Because of this, a great hunger and thirst for divine sweetness arose within the soul. 

At last, the soul began to pray properly and to humble itself in the presence of **GOD**. It began to examine and test the evil inclinations within its thoughts and to cast them aside. Through this process, the "will of reason" <note>The "will of reason" refers to human logic and ego acting independently of divine guidance.</note> was broken, and the evil, innate, or natural inclinations were gradually killed and destroyed. 

This was very distressing to the physical body, for it became faint, feeble, and weak, as if it were very sick. Yet this was no natural illness; rather, it was the "melancholy" <note>original: "melancholy"; here meaning a deep spiritual heaviness or physical exhaustion resulting from the soul's internal struggle.</note> of the body's earthly nature, occurring because its evil desires <note>original: "lusts"</note> were being destroyed.

When earthly reason found itself abandoned in this way, the poor soul saw that it was mocked and despised by the world because it would no longer walk in the way of the wicked and ungodly. Furthermore, it was inwardly assaulted by the—

<header>to true ILLUMINATION.</header>

### The Distressed Soul's Journey

This distressed Soul now began its journey <note>original: "course"</note> under the patient suffering of **CHRIST**. It entered into hope with divine confidence and grew stronger from day to day. Its evil inclinations died away more and more, so that it came to be established in a great dominion <note>original: "dominion"; a territory or sphere of influence—here, the spiritual realm of God's favor.</note> or kingdom of grace. The gates of divine revelation and the kingdom of heaven were opened and revealed within it—that is, within the Soul.

In this way, the Soul returned to its true rest through faith and became a child of **GOD** once again.

May **GOD** in His mercy help us all toward this end. **Amen.**

He must know that what Christ said is certainly true: *that he came to seek and to save that which was lost*, specifically the poor sinner who is blind and dead to <term>God</term>. He must firmly imprint this promise within himself and adopt such a strong purpose and resolution that he will not turn away from the promised grace of <term>God</term> in <term>Christ</term>, even if his body and soul were to be broken to pieces. He must persist even if he receives no comfort in his heart for the rest of his life, or the slightest assurance of the forgiveness <note>original: "remission"</note> of his sins, considering that the promises of <term>God</term> are more sure and firm than any feeling of comfort.

15. Also, let him set his purpose and lock his will so firmly in this resolution that he will never again return to his former animalistic <note>original: "beastial"</note>
